$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} {\mathbb{E}\lbrack\|\nabla f_{B_{k}}(w) - \nabla f_{B_{k}}(w^{*})\|^{2}\rbrack} & {\leq 2L\left\lbrack {f(w) - f(w^{*}) - \nabla f{(w^{*})}^{T}(w - w^{*})} \right\rbrack} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$
(4.16)

By optimality, ∇f(w*) = 0, we have


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{l} {\mathbb{E}\lbrack\|\nabla f_{B_{k}}(w) - \nabla f_{B_{k}}(w^{*})\|^{2}\rbrack \leq 2L\left\lbrack {f(w) - f(w^{*})} \right\rbrack} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$

This proves the required lemma.

Lemma 5 (Extension of Lemma 3.4 in [138] to mini-batches). Under Assumption 1 for non-smooth regularizer, we have


$$\begin{array}{l} {\mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {\|\nabla_{B_{k}}f(w_{k}^{s}) - \nabla_{B_{k}}f(w^{*})\|^{2}} \right\rbrack \leq 2L\left\lbrack {F(w_{k}^{s}) - F(w^{*})} \right\rbrack} \\ \end{array}$$
(4.17)

Proof 4.2 From inequality 4.16, we have


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} {\mathbb{E}\lbrack\|\nabla f_{B_{k}}(w) - \nabla f_{B_{k}}(w^{*})\|^{2}\rbrack} & {\leq 2L\left\lbrack {f(w) - f(w^{*}) - \nabla f{(w^{*})}^{T}(w - w^{*})} \right\rbrack} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$
(4.18)

By optimality, there exist ξ ∈ ∂g(w*), such that, ∇F(w*) = ∇f(w*) + ξ = 0, we have


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} {\mathbb{E}\lbrack\|\nabla f_{B_{k}}(w) - \nabla f_{B_{k}}(w^{*})\|^{2}\rbrack} & {\leq 2L\left\lbrack {f(w) - f(w^{*}) + \xi^{T}(w - w^{*})} \right\rbrack} \\ & {\leq 2L\left\lbrack {f(w) - f(w^{*}) + g(w) - g(w^{*})} \right\rbrack} \\ & {\leq 2L\left\lbrack {F(w) - F(w^{*})} \right\rbrack} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$
(4.19)

second inequality follows from the convexity of g. This proves the required lemma.

Lemma 6 (Variance bound for smooth problem). Under the Assumption 1 and taking $\nabla_{B_{k}}f(w_{k}^{s}) = \frac{1}{b}{\sum\limits_{i \in B_{k}}\nabla}f_{i}(w_{k}^{s})$, $\nabla_{B_{k}^{{}^{\prime}}}f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) = \frac{1}{n}{\sum\limits_{i \in B_{k}}\nabla}f_{i}({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1})$, ${\widetilde{\mu}}^{s} = \frac{1}{n}{\sum\limits_{i = 1}^{n}\nabla}f_{i}({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1})$ and the gradient estimator, ${\widetilde{\nabla}}_{s,k} = \nabla_{B_{k}}f(w_{k}^{s}) - \nabla_{B_{k}^{{}^{\prime}}}f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) + {\widetilde{\mu}}^{s}$, then the variance satisfies the following inequality4


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} {\mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {\|{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{s,k} - \nabla f(w_{k}^{s})\|^{2}} \right\rbrack} & {\leq 8L\alpha(b)\left\lbrack {f(w_{k}^{s}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack + \frac{8L\left( {\alpha(b)m^{2} + {(m - 1)}^{2}} \right)}{m^{2}}} \\ & {\quad \times \,\left\lbrack {f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack + R^{{}^{\prime}}} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$
(4.20)

where α(b) = (n − b)/(b(n − 1)) and R′ is a constant.

Proof 4.3 First the expectation of estimator is given by


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} {\mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {\widetilde{\nabla}}_{s,k} \right\rbrack} & {= \mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {\nabla_{B_{k}}f(w_{k}^{s}) - \nabla_{B_{k}^{{}^{\prime}}}f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) + {\widetilde{\mu}}^{s}} \right\rbrack} \\ & {= \nabla f(w_{k}^{s}) - \frac{b}{n}\nabla f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) + \nabla f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1})} \\ & {= \nabla f(w_{k}^{s}) + \frac{m - 1}{m}\nabla f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}),} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$
(4.21)

second equality follows as n = mb. Now the variance bound is calculated as follows,


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} & {\mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {\|{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{s,k} - \nabla f(w_{k}^{s})\|^{2}} \right\rbrack} \\ & {= \mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {\|\nabla_{B_{k}}f(w_{k}^{s}) - \nabla_{B_{k}^{{}^{\prime}}}f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) + \nabla f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - \nabla f(w_{k}^{s})\|^{2}} \right\rbrack} \\ & {= \mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {\|\nabla_{B_{k}}f(w_{k}^{s}) - \nabla_{B_{k}}f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) + \nabla f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - \nabla f(w_{k}^{s}) + \frac{m - 1}{m}\nabla_{B_{k}}f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1})\|^{2}} \right\rbrack} \\ & {\leq 2\mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {\|\nabla_{B_{k}}f(w_{k}^{s}) - \nabla_{B_{k}}f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) + \nabla f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - \nabla f(w_{k}^{s})\|^{2}} \right\rbrack} \\ & {\quad + \,\frac{2{(m - 1)}^{2}}{m^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {\|\nabla_{B_{k}}f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1})\|^{2}} \right\rbrack} \\ & {\leq 8L\alpha(b)\left\lbrack {f(w_{k}^{s}) - f^{*} + f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack + \frac{2{(m - 1)}^{2}}{m^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {\|\nabla_{B_{k}}f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1})\|^{2}} \right\rbrack} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$
(4.22)

inequality follows from, ∥a + b∥2 ≤ 2(∥a∥2+∥b∥2) for a, b ∈ ℝd and applying the Lemma 3.


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} \text{Now,~} & {\frac{2{(m - 1)}^{2}}{m^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {\|\nabla_{B_{k}}f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1})\|^{2}} \right\rbrack} \\ & {\leq \frac{2{(m - 1)}^{2}}{m^{2}}\left\lbrack {\left. 2\mathbb{E} \right\|\left. \nabla_{B_{k}}f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - \nabla_{B_{k}}f(w^{*})\|^{2} + 2\mathbb{E} \right\|\nabla_{B_{k}}f(w^{*})\|^{2}} \right\rbrack} \\ & {\leq \frac{8L{(m - 1)}^{2}}{m^{2}}\left\lbrack {f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - f(w^{*})} \right\rbrack + R^{{}^{\prime}}} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$
(4.23)

first inequality follows from, ∥a + b∥2 ≤ 2(∥a∥2+∥b∥2) for a, b ∈ ℝd, second inequality follows from Lemma 4 and assuming 𝔼∥∇Bkf(w*)∥2 ≤ R, ∀k and where taking $R^{{}^{\prime}} = \frac{2{(m - 1)}^{2}}{m^{2}}*R$. Now, substituting the above inequality in 4.22, we have


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} & {\mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {\|{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{s,k} - \nabla f(w_{k}^{s})\|^{2}} \right\rbrack} \\ & {\leq 8L\alpha(b)\left\lbrack {f(w_{k}^{s}) - f^{*} + f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack + \frac{8L{(m - 1)}^{2}}{m^{2}}\left\lbrack {f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - f(w^{*})} \right\rbrack + R^{{}^{\prime}}} \\ & {= 8L\alpha(b)\left\lbrack {f(w_{k}^{s}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack + \frac{8L\left( {\alpha(b)m^{2} + {(m - 1)}^{2}} \right)}{m^{2}}\left\lbrack {f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack + R^{{}^{\prime}},} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$
(4.24)

This proves the required lemma.

Lemma 7 (Variance bound for non-smooth problem). Under Assumption 1 and taking notations as in Lemma 6, the variance bound satisfies the following inequality,


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} {\mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {\|{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{s,k} - \nabla f(w_{k}^{s})\|^{2}} \right\rbrack} & {\leq 8L\alpha(b)\left\lbrack {F(w_{k}^{s}) - F^{*}} \right\rbrack + \frac{8L\left( {\alpha(b)m^{2} + {(m - 1)}^{2}} \right)}{m^{2}}} \\ & {\quad \times \,\left\lbrack {F({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - F^{*}} \right\rbrack + R^{{}^{\prime}},} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$
(4.25)

where α(b) = (n − b)/(b(n − 1)) and R′ is constant.

Proof 4.4


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} & {\mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {\|{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{s,k} - \nabla f(w_{k}^{s})\|^{2}} \right\rbrack} \\ & {= \mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {\|\nabla_{B_{k}}f(w_{k}^{s}) - \nabla_{B_{k}^{{}^{\prime}}}f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) + \nabla f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - \nabla f(w_{k}^{s})\|^{2}} \right\rbrack} \\ & {= \mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {\|\nabla_{B_{k}}f(w_{k}^{s}) - \nabla_{B_{k}}f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) + \nabla f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - \nabla f(w_{k}^{s}) + \frac{m - 1}{m}\nabla_{B_{k}}f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1})\|^{2}} \right\rbrack} \\ & {\leq 2\mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {\|\nabla_{B_{k}}f(w_{k}^{s}) - \nabla_{B_{k}}f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) + \nabla f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - \nabla f(w_{k}^{s})\|^{2}} \right\rbrack} \\ & {\quad + \,\frac{2{(m - 1)}^{2}}{m^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {\|\nabla_{B_{k}}f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1})\|^{2}} \right\rbrack} \\ & {\leq 8L\alpha(b)\left\lbrack {F(w_{k}^{s}) - F(w^{*}) + F({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - F(w^{*})} \right\rbrack + \frac{2{(m - 1)}^{2}}{m^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {\|\nabla_{B_{k}}f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1})\|^{2}} \right\rbrack} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$
(4.26)

inequality follows from, ∥a + b∥2 ≤ 2(∥a∥2+∥b∥2) for a, b ∈ ℝd and applying the Lemma 2.


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} & {\text{Now,~}\frac{2{(m - 1)}^{2}}{m^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {\|\nabla_{B_{k}}f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1})\|^{2}} \right\rbrack} \\ & {\leq \frac{2{(m - 1)}^{2}}{m^{2}}\left\lbrack {\left. 2\mathbb{E} \right\|\left. \nabla_{B_{k}}f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - \nabla_{B_{k}}f(w^{*})\|^{2} + 2\mathbb{E} \right\|\nabla_{B_{k}}f(w^{*})\|^{2}} \right\rbrack} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$
(4.27)


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} & {\leq \frac{8L{(m - 1)}^{2}}{m^{2}}\left\lbrack {F({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - F(w^{*})} \right\rbrack + R^{{}^{\prime}}} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$
(4.28)

first inequality follows from, ∥a + b∥2 ≤ 2(∥a∥2+∥b∥2) for a, b ∈ ℝd, second inequality follows from Lemma 5 and assuming ∥∇Bkf(w*)∥2 ≤ R, ∀k and taking $R^{{}^{\prime}} = \frac{2{(m - 1)}^{2}}{m^{2}}*R$. Now, substituting the above inequality in 4.26, we have


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} & {\mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {\|{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{s,k} - \nabla f(w_{k}^{s})\|^{2}} \right\rbrack} \\ & {\leq 8L\alpha(b)\left\lbrack {F(w_{k}^{s}) - F^{*} + F({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - F^{*}} \right\rbrack + \frac{8L{(m - 1)}^{2}}{m^{2}}\left\lbrack {F({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - F(w^{*})} \right\rbrack + R^{{}^{\prime}}} \\ & {= 8L\alpha(b)\left\lbrack {F(w_{k}^{s}) - F^{*}} \right\rbrack + \frac{8L\left( {\alpha(b)m^{2} + {(m - 1)}^{2}} \right)}{m^{2}}\left\lbrack {F({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - F^{*}} \right\rbrack + R^{{}^{\prime}},} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$
(4.29)

This proves the required lemma.

Theorem 2 Under the assumptions of Lipschitz continuity with smooth regularizer, the convergence of SAAG-IV is given below:


$$\begin{array}{l} {\mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {f({\widetilde{w}}^{s}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack \leq C^{s}\left\lbrack {f({\widetilde{w}}^{0}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack + V,} \\ \end{array}$$
(4.30)

where, $C = \left\lbrack {\frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1 - 4\alpha(b))}\frac{c}{m} + \frac{4\left( {\alpha(b)m^{2} + {(m - 1)}^{2}} \right)}{m^{2}(\beta - 1 - 4\alpha(b))}} \right\rbrack < 1$ and V is constant.

Proof 4.5 By smoothness, we have,


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} {f(w_{k + 1}^{s})} & {\leq f(w_{k}^{s}) + < \nabla f(w_{k}^{s}),w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s} > + \frac{L}{2}\| w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2}} \\ & {= f(w_{k}^{s}) + < \nabla f(w_{k}^{s}),w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s} > + \frac{L\beta}{2}\| w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2}} \\ & {\quad - \,\frac{L(\beta - 1)}{2}\| w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2}} \\ & {= f(w_{k}^{s}) + < {\widetilde{\nabla}}_{s,k},w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s} > + \frac{L\beta}{2}\| w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2} + < \nabla f(w_{k}^{s}) -} \\ & {\quad - \,{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{s,k},w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s} > - \frac{L(\beta - 1}{2}\| w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2},} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$
(4.31)

where β is appropriately chosen positive value. Now, separately simplifying the terms, we have


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} & {\mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {f(w_{k}^{s}) + < {\widetilde{\nabla}}_{s,k},w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s} > + \frac{L\beta}{2}\| w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2}} \right\rbrack} \\ & {= f(w_{k}^{s}) + \mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {< {\widetilde{\nabla}}_{s,k},w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s} >} \right\rbrack + \frac{L\beta}{2}\| w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2}} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} & {= f(w_{k}^{s}) + < \nabla f(w_{k}^{s}) + \frac{m - 1}{m}\nabla f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}),w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s} > + \frac{L\beta}{2}\| w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2}} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} & {= f(w_{k}^{s}) + < \nabla f(w_{k}^{s}),w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s} > + \frac{L\beta}{2}\| w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2} + \frac{m - 1}{m}} \\ & {< \nabla f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}),w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s} >} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} & {\leq f(w_{k}^{s}) + < \nabla f(w_{k}^{s}),w^{*} - w_{k}^{s} > + \frac{L\beta}{2}\| w^{*} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2} - \frac{L\beta}{2}\| w^{*} - w_{k + 1}^{s}\|^{2}} \\ & {\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\, + \frac{m - 1}{m} < \nabla f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}),w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s} >} \\ & {= f(w_{k}^{s}) + < \nabla f(w_{k}^{s}),w^{*} - w_{k}^{s} > + \frac{L\beta}{2}\| w^{*} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2} - \frac{L\beta}{2}\| w^{*} - w_{k + 1}^{s}\|^{2}} \\ & {\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\, + \frac{m - 1}{m}\left\lbrack {< \nabla f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}),w_{k + 1}^{s} - w^{*} > - < \nabla f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}),w_{k}^{s} - w^{*} >} \right\rbrack} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} & {\leq f(w^{*}) + \frac{L\beta}{2}\left\lbrack {\| w^{*} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2} - \| w^{*} - w_{k + 1}^{s}\|^{2}} \right\rbrack} \\ & {\quad + \,\frac{m - 1}{m}\left\lbrack {\frac{1}{2\delta}\|\nabla f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1})\|^{2} + \frac{\delta}{2}\| w_{k + 1}^{s} - w^{*}\|^{2}}~ \right.} \\ & {\quad - \,\left. ~\left\lbrack {\frac{1}{2\delta}\|\nabla f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1})\|^{2} + \frac{\delta}{2}\| w_{k}^{s} - w^{*}\|^{2}} \right\rbrack \right\rbrack} \\ & {= f(w^{*}) + \frac{L\beta}{2}\left\lbrack {\| w^{*} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2} - \| w^{*} - w_{k + 1}^{s}\|^{2}} \right\rbrack} \\ & {\quad + \,\frac{\delta(m - 1)}{2m}\left\lbrack {\| w_{k + 1}^{s} - w^{*}\|^{2} - \| w_{k}^{s} - w^{*}\|^{2}} \right\rbrack,} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} & {= f(w^{*}) + \left( {\frac{L\beta}{2} - \frac{\delta(m - 1)}{2m}} \right)\left\lbrack {\| w^{*} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2} - \| w^{*} - w_{k + 1}^{s}\|^{2}} \right\rbrack,} \\ & {= f(w^{*}),} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$
(4.32)

second equality follows from, $\mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {\widetilde{\nabla}}_{s,k} \right\rbrack = \nabla f(w_{k}^{s}) + \frac{m - 1}{m}\nabla f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1})$, first inequality follows from Lemma 1, second inequality follows from the convexity, i.e., f(w*) ≥ f(wks) +  < ∇f(wks), w* − wks> and Young's inequality, i.e., xTy ≤ 1/(2δ)∥x∥2 + δ/2∥y∥2 for δ > 0, and last equality follows by choosing $\delta = \frac{mL\beta}{(m - 1)}$.


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} & {\text{and},\,\mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {< \nabla f(w_{k}^{s}) - {\widetilde{\nabla}}_{s,k},w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s} > - \frac{L(\beta - 1)}{2}\| w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2}} \right\rbrack} \\ & {\leq \mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {\frac{1}{2L(\beta - 1)}\|\nabla f(w_{k}^{s}) - {\widetilde{\nabla}}_{s,k}\|^{2} + \frac{L(\beta - 1)}{2}\| w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2} - \frac{L(\beta - 1)}{2}\| w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2}} \right\rbrack} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} & {= \frac{1}{2L(\beta - 1)}\mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {\|{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{s,k} - \nabla f(w_{k}^{s})\|^{2}} \right\rbrack} \\ & {\leq \frac{1}{2L(\beta - 1)}\left\lbrack {8L\alpha(b)\left\lbrack {f(w_{k}^{s}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack + \frac{8L\left( {\alpha(b)m^{2} + {(m - 1)}^{2}} \right)}{m^{2}}\left\lbrack {f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack + R^{{}^{\prime}}} \right\rbrack} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} & {= \frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)}\left\lbrack {f(w_{k}^{s}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack + \frac{4\left( {\alpha(b)m^{2} + {(m - 1)}^{2}} \right)}{m^{2}(\beta - 1)}\left\lbrack {f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack + R^{{}^{\prime\prime}}} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$
(4.33)

first inequality follows from Young's inequality, second inequality follows from Lemma 6 and R′′ = R′/(2L(β − 1)). Now, substituting the values into 4.31 from inequalities 4.32 and 4.33, and taking expectation w.r.t. mini-batches, we have


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} & {\mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {f(w_{k + 1}^{s})} \right\rbrack \leq f(w^{*}) + \frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)}\left\lbrack {f(w_{k}^{s}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack + \frac{4\left( {\alpha(b)m^{2} + {(m - 1)}^{2}} \right)}{m^{2}(\beta - 1)}\left\lbrack {f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack + R^{{}^{\prime\prime}}} \\ & {\mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {f(w_{k + 1}^{s}) - f(w^{*})} \right\rbrack \leq \frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)}\left\lbrack {f(w_{k}^{s}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack + \frac{4\left( {\alpha(b)m^{2} + {(m - 1)}^{2}} \right)}{m^{2}(\beta - 1)}\left\lbrack {f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack + R^{{}^{\prime\prime}}} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$
(4.34)

Taking sum over k = 0, 1, ..., (m − 1) and dividing by m, we have


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} & {\frac{1}{m}{\sum\limits_{k = 0}^{m - 1}\mathbb{E}}\left\lbrack {f(w_{k + 1}^{s}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack} \\ & {\leq \frac{1}{m}{\sum\limits_{k = 0}^{m - 1}\left\lbrack {\frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)}\left\lbrack {f(w_{k}^{s}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack + \frac{4\left( {\alpha(b)m^{2} + {(m - 1)}^{2}} \right)}{m^{2}(\beta - 1)}\left\lbrack {f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack + R^{{}^{\prime\prime}}} \right\rbrack},} \\ & {\frac{1}{m}{\sum\limits_{k = 1}^{m}\mathbb{E}}\left\lbrack {f(w_{k}^{s}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack} \\ & {\leq \frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)}\frac{1}{m}{\sum\limits_{k = 1}^{m}\left\lbrack {f(w_{k}^{s}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack} + \frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)}\frac{1}{m}\left\lbrack {f(w_{0}^{s}) - f^{*} - \left\{ f(w_{m}^{s}) - f^{*} \right\}} \right\rbrack} \\ & {\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\, + \frac{4\left( {\alpha(b)m^{2} + {(m - 1)}^{2}} \right)}{m^{2}(\beta - 1)}\left\lbrack {f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack + R^{{}^{\prime\prime}}} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$
(4.35)

Subtracting $\frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)}\frac{1}{m}{\sum\limits_{k = 1}^{m}\left\lbrack {f(w_{k}^{s}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack}$ from both sides, we have


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} & {\left( {1 - \frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)}} \right)\frac{1}{m}{\sum\limits_{k = 1}^{m}\mathbb{E}}\left\lbrack {f(w_{k}^{s}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack} \\ & {\leq \frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)}\frac{1}{m}\left\lbrack {f(w_{0}^{s}) - f^{*} - \left\{ f(w_{m}^{s}) - f^{*} \right\}} \right\rbrack + \frac{4\left( {\alpha(b)m^{2} + {(m - 1)}^{2}} \right)}{m^{2}(\beta - 1)}\left\lbrack {f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack + R^{{}^{\prime\prime}}} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$
(4.36)

Since f(wms) − f* ≥ 0 so dropping this term and using Assumption 3, we have


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} & {\left( {1 - \frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)}} \right)\frac{1}{m}{\sum\limits_{k = 1}^{m}\mathbb{E}}\left\lbrack {f(w_{k}^{s}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack} \\ & {\leq \frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)}\frac{1}{m}\left\lbrack {f(w_{0}^{s}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack + \frac{4\left( {\alpha(b)m^{2} + {(m - 1)}^{2}} \right)}{m^{2}(\beta - 1)}\left\lbrack {f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack + R^{{}^{\prime\prime}}} \\ & {\leq \frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)}\frac{1}{m}\left\lbrack {c\left( {f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - f^{*}} \right)} \right\rbrack + \frac{4\left( {\alpha(b)m^{2} + {(m - 1)}^{2}} \right)}{m^{2}(\beta - 1)}\left\lbrack {f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack + R^{{}^{\prime\prime}}} \\ & {= \left\lbrack {\frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)}\frac{c}{m} + \frac{4\left( {\alpha(b)m^{2} + {(m - 1)}^{2}} \right)}{m^{2}(\beta - 1)}} \right\rbrack\left\lbrack \left( {f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - f^{*}} \right) \right\rbrack + R^{{}^{\prime\prime}},} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$
(4.37)

Dividing both sides by $\left( {1 - \frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)}} \right)$, and since ${\widetilde{w}}^{s} = 1/m{\sum\limits_{k = 1}^{m}w_{k}^{s}}$ so by convexity, $f({\widetilde{w}}^{s}) \leq 1/m{\sum\limits_{k = 1}^{m}f}(w_{k}^{s})$, we have


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{l} {\mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {f({\widetilde{w}}^{s}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack \leq \left\lbrack {\frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1 - 4\alpha(b))}\frac{c}{m} + \frac{4\left( {\alpha(b)m^{2} + {(m - 1)}^{2}} \right)}{m^{2}(\beta - 1 - 4\alpha(b))}} \right\rbrack\left\lbrack {f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack + R^{{}^{\prime\prime\prime}},} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$
(4.38)

where R′′′ = R′′(β − 1)/(β−1−4α(b)). Now, applying this inequality recursively, we have


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{l} {\mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {f({\widetilde{w}}^{s}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack \leq C^{s}\left\lbrack {f({\widetilde{w}}^{0}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack + R^{{}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}},} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$
(4.39)

inequality follows for R′′′′ = R′′′/(1 − C), since ${\sum\limits_{i = 0}^{k}r^{i}} \leq {\sum\limits_{i = 0}^{\infty}r^{i}} = \frac{1}{1 - r},\quad\| r\| < 1$ and $C = \left\lbrack {\frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1 - 4\alpha(b))}\frac{c}{m} + \frac{4\left( {\alpha(b)m^{2} + {(m - 1)}^{2}} \right)}{m^{2}(\beta - 1 - 4\alpha(b))}} \right\rbrack$. For certain choice of β, one can easily prove that C < 1. This proves linear convergence with some initial error.

Theorem 3 Under the assumptions of Lipschitz continuity and strong convexity with smooth regularizer, the convergence of SAAG-IV method is given below:


$$\begin{array}{l} {\mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {f({\widetilde{w}}^{s}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack \leq C^{s}\left\lbrack {f({\widetilde{w}}^{0}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack + V,} \\ \end{array}$$
(4.40)

where, $C = \left\lbrack {\frac{cL\beta}{m\mu} + \frac{4\left( {\alpha(b)m^{2} + {(m - 1)}^{2}} \right)}{m^{2}(\beta - 1)} - \frac{c(m - 1)}{m^{2}} + \frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)}} \right\rbrack\text{\textbackslash\textbackslash}\left( {1 - \frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)} - c\left( {\frac{m - 1}{m^{2}} - \frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)}\frac{1}{m}} \right)} \right)^{- 1} < 1$ and V is constant.

Proof 4.6 By smoothness, we have,


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} {f(w_{k + 1}^{s})} & {\leq f(w_{k}^{s}) + < \nabla f(w_{k}^{s}),w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s} > + \frac{L}{2}\| w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2}} \\ & {= f(w_{k}^{s}) + < \nabla f(w_{k}^{s}),w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s} > + \frac{L\beta}{2}\| w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2} - \frac{L(\beta - 1}{2}\| w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2}} \\ & {= f(w_{k}^{s}) + < {\widetilde{\nabla}}_{s,k},w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s} > + \frac{L\beta}{2}\| w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2} + < \nabla f(w_{k}^{s})} \\ & {\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\, - {\widetilde{\nabla}}_{s,k},w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s} > - \frac{L(\beta - 1}{2}\| w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2}} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$
(4.41)

where β is appropriately chosen positive value. Now, separately simplifying the terms, we have


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} & {\mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {f(w_{k}^{s}) + < {\widetilde{\nabla}}_{s,k},w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s} > + \frac{L\beta}{2}\| w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2}} \right\rbrack} \\ & {= f(w_{k}^{s}) + \mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {< {\widetilde{\nabla}}_{s,k},w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s} >} \right\rbrack + \frac{L\beta}{2}\| w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2}} \\ & {= f(w_{k}^{s}) + < \nabla f(w_{k}^{s}) + \frac{m - 1}{m}\nabla f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}),w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s} > + \frac{L\beta}{2}\| w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2}} \\ & {= f(w_{k}^{s}) + < \nabla f(w_{k}^{s}),w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s} > + \frac{L\beta}{2}\| w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2} + \frac{m - 1}{m}} \\ & {< \nabla f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}),w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s} >} \\ & {\leq f(w_{k}^{s}) + < \nabla f(w_{k}^{s}),w^{*} - w_{k}^{s} > + \frac{L\beta}{2}\| w^{*} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2} - \frac{L\beta}{2}\| w^{*} - w_{k + 1}^{s}\|^{2}} \\ & {\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\, + \frac{m - 1}{m} < \nabla f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}),w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s} >} \\ & {= f(w_{k}^{s}) + < \nabla f(w_{k}^{s}),w^{*} - w_{k}^{s} > + \frac{L\beta}{2}\| w^{*} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2} - \frac{L\beta}{2}\| w^{*} - w_{k + 1}^{s}\|^{2}} \\ & {\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\, + \frac{m - 1}{m}\left\lbrack {< \nabla f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}),w_{k + 1}^{s} - {\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1} > - < \nabla f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}),w_{k}^{s} - {\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1} >} \right\rbrack} \\ & {\leq f(w^{*}) + \frac{L\beta}{2}\left\lbrack {\| w^{*} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2} - \| w^{*} - w_{k + 1}^{s}\|^{2}} \right\rbrack} \\ & {\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\, + \frac{m - 1}{m}\left\lbrack {f(w_{k + 1}^{s}) - f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - \left( {f(w_{k}^{s}) - f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1})} \right)} \right\rbrack} \\ & {= f(w^{*}) + \frac{L\beta}{2}\left\lbrack {\| w^{*} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2} - \| w^{*} - w_{k + 1}^{s}\|^{2}} \right\rbrack + \frac{m - 1}{m}\left\lbrack {f(w_{k + 1}^{s}) - f(w_{k}^{s})} \right\rbrack,} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$
(4.42)

second equality follows from, $\mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {\widetilde{\nabla}}_{s,k} \right\rbrack = \nabla f(w_{k}^{s}) + \frac{m - 1}{m}\nabla f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1})$, first inequality follows from Lemma 1 and second inequality follows from the convexity, i.e., f(x) ≥ f(y) +  < ∇f(y), x − y>.


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} & {\text{and},\,\mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {< \nabla f(w_{k}^{s}) - {\widetilde{\nabla}}_{s,k},w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s} > - \frac{L(\beta - 1)}{2}\| w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2}} \right\rbrack} \\ & {\leq \mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {\frac{1}{2L(\beta - 1)}\|\nabla f(w_{k}^{s}) - {\widetilde{\nabla}}_{s,k}\|^{2} + \frac{L(\beta - 1)}{2}\| w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2} - \frac{L(\beta - 1)}{2}\| w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2}} \right\rbrack} \\ & {\leq \frac{1}{2L(\beta - 1)}\left\lbrack {8L\alpha(b)\left\lbrack {f(w_{k}^{s}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack + \frac{8L\left( {\alpha(b)m^{2} + {(m - 1)}^{2}} \right)}{m^{2}}\left\lbrack {f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack + R^{{}^{\prime}}} \right\rbrack} \\ & {= \frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)}\left\lbrack {f(w_{k}^{s}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack + \frac{4\left( {\alpha(b)m^{2} + {(m - 1)}^{2}} \right)}{m^{2}(\beta - 1)}\left\lbrack {f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack + R^{{}^{\prime\prime}}} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$
(4.43)

first inequality follows from Young's inequality and second inequality follows from Lemma 6 and R′′ = R′/(2L(β − 1)).

Now, substituting the values into 4.41 from inequalities 4.42 and 4.43, and taking expectation w.r.t. mini-batches, we have


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} & {\mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {f(w_{k + 1}^{s})} \right\rbrack} \\ & {\leq f(w^{*}) + \frac{L\beta}{2}\left\lbrack {\| w^{*} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2} - \| w^{*} - w_{k + 1}^{s}\|^{2}} \right\rbrack + \frac{m - 1}{m}\left\lbrack {f(w_{k + 1}^{s}) - f(w_{k}^{s})} \right\rbrack} \\ & {\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\, + \frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)}\left\lbrack {f(w_{k}^{s}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack + \frac{4\left( {\alpha(b)m^{2} + {(m - 1)}^{2}} \right)}{m^{2}(\beta - 1)}\left\lbrack {f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack + R^{{}^{\prime\prime}},} \\ & {\mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {f(w_{k + 1}^{s}) - f(w^{*})} \right\rbrack} \\ & {= \frac{L\beta}{2}\left\lbrack {\| w^{*} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2} - \| w^{*} - w_{k + 1}^{s}\|^{2}} \right\rbrack + \frac{m - 1}{m}\left\lbrack {f(w_{k + 1}^{s}) - f(w_{k}^{s})} \right\rbrack} \\ & {\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\, + \frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)}\left\lbrack {f(w_{k}^{s}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack + \frac{4\left( {\alpha(b)m^{2} + {(m - 1)}^{2}} \right)}{m^{2}(\beta - 1)}\left\lbrack {f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack + R^{{}^{\prime\prime}}} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$
(4.44)

Taking sum over k = 0, 1, ..., (m − 1) and dividing by m, we have


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} & {\frac{1}{m}{\sum\limits_{k = 0}^{m - 1}\mathbb{E}}\left\lbrack {f(w_{k + 1}^{s}) - f(w^{*})} \right\rbrack} \\ & {\leq \frac{1}{m}{\sum\limits_{k = 0}^{m - 1}\left\{ {\frac{L\beta}{2}\left\lbrack {\| w^{*} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2} - \| w^{*} - w_{k + 1}^{s}\|^{2}} \right\rbrack + \frac{m - 1}{m}\left\lbrack {f(w_{k + 1}^{s}) - f(w_{k}^{s})} \right\rbrack} \right\}}} \\ & {\quad + \,\frac{1}{m}{\sum\limits_{k = 0}^{m - 1}\left\{ {\frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)}\left\lbrack {f(w_{k}^{s}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack + \frac{4\left( {\alpha(b)m^{2} + {(m - 1)}^{2}} \right)}{m^{2}(\beta - 1)}\left\lbrack {f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack + R^{{}^{\prime\prime}}} \right\}},} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} & {\frac{1}{m}{\sum\limits_{k = 1}^{m}\mathbb{E}}\left\lbrack {f(w_{k}^{s}) - f(w^{*})} \right\rbrack} \\ & {\leq \frac{L\beta}{2m}\left\lbrack {\| w^{*} - w_{0}^{s}\|^{2} - \| w^{*} - w_{m}^{s}\|^{2}} \right\rbrack + \frac{m - 1}{m^{2}}\left\lbrack {f(w_{m}^{s}) - f(w_{0}^{s})} \right\rbrack} \\ & {\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\, + \frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)}\frac{1}{m}\left\{ {{\sum\limits_{k = 1}^{m}\left\lbrack {f(w_{k}^{s}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack} + f(w_{0}^{s}) - f^{*} - \left( {f(w_{m}^{s}) - f^{*}} \right)} \right\}} \\ & {\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\, + \frac{4\left( {\alpha(b)m^{2} + {(m - 1)}^{2}} \right)}{m^{2}(\beta - 1)}\left\lbrack {f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack + R^{{}^{\prime\prime}}} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$
(4.45)

Subtracting $\frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)}\frac{1}{m}{\sum\limits_{k = 1}^{m}\left\lbrack {f(w_{k}^{s}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack}$ from both sides, we have


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} & {\left( {1 - \frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)}} \right)\frac{1}{m}{\sum\limits_{k = 1}^{m}\mathbb{E}}\left\lbrack {f(w_{k}^{s}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack} \\ & {\leq \frac{L\beta}{2m}\left\lbrack {\| w^{*} - w_{0}^{s}\|^{2} - \| w^{*} - w_{m}^{s}\|^{2}} \right\rbrack - \left( {\frac{m - 1}{m^{2}} - \frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)}\frac{1}{m}} \right)\left\lbrack {f(w_{0}^{s}) - f(w_{m}^{s})} \right\rbrack} \\ & {\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\, + \frac{4\left( {\alpha(b)m^{2} + {(m - 1)}^{2}} \right)}{m^{2}(\beta - 1)}\left\lbrack {f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack + R^{{}^{\prime\prime}}} \\ & {\leq \frac{L\beta}{2m}\| w^{*} - w_{0}^{s}\|^{2} - \left( {\frac{m - 1}{m^{2}} - \frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)}\frac{1}{m}} \right)\left\lbrack {f(w_{0}^{s}) - f^{*} - \left\{ f(w_{m}^{s}) - f^{*} \right\}} \right\rbrack} \\ & {\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\, + \frac{4\left( {\alpha(b)m^{2} + {(m - 1)}^{2}} \right)}{m^{2}(\beta - 1)}\left\lbrack {f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack + R^{{}^{\prime\prime}}} \\ & {\leq \frac{L\beta}{2m}\frac{2}{\mu}\left( {f(w_{0}^{s}) - f^{*}} \right) - \left( {\frac{m - 1}{m^{2}} - \frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)}\frac{1}{m}} \right)\left\lbrack {c\left\lbrack {f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack - c\left\lbrack {f({\widetilde{w}}^{s}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack} \right\rbrack} \\ & {\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\, + \frac{4\left( {\alpha(b)m^{2} + {(m - 1)}^{2}} \right)}{m^{2}(\beta - 1)}\left\lbrack {f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack + R^{{}^{\prime\prime}}} \\ & {\leq \frac{L\beta}{2m}\frac{2}{\mu}c\left\lbrack {f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack - \left( {\frac{m - 1}{m^{2}} - \frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)}\frac{1}{m}} \right)\left\lbrack {c\left\lbrack {f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack - c\left\lbrack {f({\widetilde{w}}^{s}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack} \right\rbrack} \\ & {\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\, + \frac{4\left( {\alpha(b)m^{2} + {(m - 1)}^{2}} \right)}{m^{2}(\beta - 1)}\left\lbrack {f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack + R^{{}^{\prime\prime}}} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$
(4.46)

second inequality follows by dropping, ∥w* − wms∥2 > 0, third inequality follows from the strong convexity, i.e., ∥w0s − w*∥2 ≤ 2/μ(f(w0s)−f*) and application of Assumption 3 twice, and fourth inequality follows from Assumption 3. Since ${\widetilde{w}}^{s} = 1/m{\sum\limits_{k = 1}^{m}w_{k}^{s}}$ so by convexity using, $f({\widetilde{w}}^{s}) \leq 1/m{\sum\limits_{k = 1}^{m}f}(w_{k}^{s})$, we have


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} & {\left( {1 - \frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)}} \right)\mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {f({\widetilde{w}}^{s}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack} \\ & {\leq \frac{L\beta}{2m}\frac{2}{\mu}c\left\lbrack {f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack - \left( {\frac{m - 1}{m^{2}} - \frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)}\frac{1}{m}} \right)\left\lbrack {c\left\lbrack {f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack - c\left\lbrack {f({\widetilde{w}}^{s}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack} \right\rbrack} \\ & {\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\, + \frac{4\left( {\alpha(b)m^{2} + {(m - 1)}^{2}} \right)}{m^{2}(\beta - 1)}\left\lbrack {f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack + R^{{}^{\prime\prime}}} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$
(4.47)

Subtracting, $c\left( {\frac{m - 1}{m^{2}} - \frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)}\frac{1}{m}} \right)\mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {f({\widetilde{w}}^{s}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack$ both sides, we have


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} & {\left( {1 - \frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)} - c\left( {\frac{m - 1}{m^{2}} - \frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)}\frac{1}{m}} \right)} \right)\mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {f({\widetilde{w}}^{s}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack} \\ & {\leq \left\lbrack {\frac{cL\beta}{m\mu} + \frac{4\left( {\alpha(b)m^{2} + {(m - 1)}^{2}} \right)}{m^{2}(\beta - 1)} - \frac{c(m - 1)}{m^{2}} + \frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)}} \right\rbrack\left\lbrack {f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack + R^{{}^{\prime\prime}}} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$
(4.48)

Dividing both sides by $\left( {1 - \frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)} - c\left( {\frac{m - 1}{m^{2}} - \frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)}\frac{1}{m}} \right)} \right)$, we have


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{l} {\mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {f({\widetilde{w}}^{s}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack \leq C\left\lbrack {f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack + R^{{}^{\prime\prime\prime}}} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$
(4.49)

where C = $\left\lbrack {\frac{cL\beta}{m\mu} + \frac{4\left( {\alpha(b)m^{2} + {(m - 1)}^{2}} \right)}{m^{2}(\beta - 1)} - \frac{c(m - 1)}{m^{2}} + \frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)}} \right\rbrack$ $\left( {1 - \frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)} - c\left( {\frac{m - 1}{m^{2}} - \frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)}\frac{1}{m}} \right)} \right)^{- 1}$ and $R^{{}^{\prime\prime\prime}} = R^{{}^{\prime\prime}}\left( {1 - \frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)} - c\left( {\frac{m - 1}{m^{2}} - \frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)}\frac{1}{m}} \right)} \right)^{- 1}$. Now, recursively applying the inequality, we have


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{l} {\mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {f({\widetilde{w}}^{s}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack \leq C^{s}\left\lbrack {f({\widetilde{w}}^{0}) - f^{*}} \right\rbrack + R^{{}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}},} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$
(4.50)

inequality follows for R′′′′ = R′′′/(1 − C), since ${\sum\limits_{i = 0}^{k}r^{i}} \leq {\sum\limits_{i = 0}^{\infty}r^{i}} = \frac{1}{1 - r},\quad\| r\| < 1$. For certain choice of β, one can easily prove that C < 1. This proves linear convergence with some initial error.

Theorem 4 Under the assumptions of Lipschitz continuity with non-smooth regularizer, the convergence of SAAG-IV is given below:


$$\begin{array}{l} {\mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {F({\widetilde{w}}^{s}) - F(w^{*})} \right\rbrack \leq C^{s}\left\lbrack {F({\widetilde{w}}^{0}) - F(w^{*})} \right\rbrack + V,} \\ \end{array}$$
(4.51)

where, $C = \left( {\frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1 - 4\alpha(b))}\frac{c}{m} + \frac{4\left( {\alpha(b)m^{2} + {(m - 1)}^{2}} \right)}{m^{2}(\beta - 1 - 4\alpha(b))}} \right) < 1$ and V is constant.

Proof 4.7 By smoothness, we have,


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} {f(w_{k + 1}^{s})} & {\leq f(w_{k}^{s}) + < \nabla f(w_{k}^{s}),w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s} > + \frac{L}{2}\| w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2}} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$
(4.52)


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} \text{Now,~} & {F(w_{k + 1}^{s}) = f(w_{k + 1}^{s}) + g(w_{k + 1}^{s})} \\ & {\leq f(w_{k}^{s}) + g(w_{k + 1}^{s}) + < \nabla f(w_{k}^{s}),w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s} > + \frac{L}{2}\| w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2}} \\ & {= f(w_{k}^{s}) + g(w_{k + 1}^{s}) + < \nabla f(w_{k}^{s}),w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s} > + \frac{L\beta}{2}\| w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2}} \\ & {\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\, - \frac{L(\beta - 1}{2}\| w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2}} \\ & {= f(w_{k}^{s}) + g(w_{k + 1}^{s}) + < {\widetilde{\nabla}}_{s,k},w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s} > + \frac{L\beta}{2}\| w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2} +} \\ & {\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\, < \nabla f(w_{k}^{s}) - {\widetilde{\nabla}}_{s,k},w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s} > - \frac{L(\beta - 1}{2}\| w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2}} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$
(4.53)

where β is appropriately chosen positive value. Now, separately simplifying the terms, we have


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} & {\mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {f(w_{k}^{s}) + g(w_{k + 1}^{s}) + < {\widetilde{\nabla}}_{s,k},w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s} > + \frac{L\beta}{2}\| w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2}} \right\rbrack} \\ & {= f(w_{k}^{s}) + g(w_{k + 1}^{s}) + \mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {< {\widetilde{\nabla}}_{s,k},w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s} >} \right\rbrack + \frac{L\beta}{2}\| w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2}} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} & {= f(w_{k}^{s}) + g(w_{k + 1}^{s}) + < \nabla f(w_{k}^{s}) + \frac{m - 1}{m}\nabla f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}),w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s} > + \frac{L\beta}{2}\| w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2}} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} & {= f(w_{k}^{s}) + g(w_{k + 1}^{s}) + < \nabla f(w_{k}^{s}),w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s} > + \frac{L\beta}{2}\| w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2}} \\ & {\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\, + \frac{m - 1}{m} < \nabla f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}),w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s} >} \\ & {\leq f(w_{k}^{s}) + g(w^{*}) + < \nabla f(w_{k}^{s}),w^{*} - w_{k}^{s} > + \frac{L\beta}{2}\| w^{*} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2} - \frac{L\beta}{2}\| w^{*} - w_{k + 1}^{s}\|^{2}} \\ & {\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\, + \frac{m - 1}{m} < \nabla f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}),w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s} >} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} & {= f(w_{k}^{s}) + g(w^{*}) + < \nabla f(w_{k}^{s}),w^{*} - w_{k}^{s} > + \frac{L\beta}{2}\| w^{*} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2} - \frac{L\beta}{2}\| w^{*} - w_{k + 1}^{s}\|^{2}} \\ & {\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\, + \frac{m - 1}{m}\left\lbrack {< \nabla f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}),w_{k + 1}^{s} - w^{*} > - < \nabla f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}),w_{k}^{s} - w^{*} >} \right\rbrack} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} & {\leq f(w^{*}) + g(w^{*}) + \frac{L\beta}{2}\left\lbrack {\| w^{*} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2} - \| w^{*} - w_{k + 1}^{s}\|^{2}} \right\rbrack} \\ & {\quad + \,\frac{m - 1}{m}\left\lbrack {\frac{1}{2\delta}\|\nabla f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1})\|^{2} + \frac{\delta}{2}\| w_{k + 1}^{s} - w^{*}\|^{2} - \left\lbrack {\frac{1}{2\delta}\|\nabla f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1})\|^{2} + \frac{\delta}{2}\| w_{k}^{s} - w^{*}\|^{2}} \right\rbrack} \right\rbrack} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} & {= F(w^{*}) + \frac{L\beta}{2}\left\lbrack {\| w^{*} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2} - \| w^{*} - w_{k + 1}^{s}\|^{2}} \right\rbrack + \frac{\delta(m - 1)}{2m}\left\lbrack {\| w_{k + 1}^{s} - w^{*}\|^{2} - \| w_{k}^{s} - w^{*}\|^{2}} \right\rbrack,} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} & {= F(w^{*}) + \left( {\frac{L\beta}{2} - \frac{\delta(m - 1)}{2m}} \right)\left\lbrack {\| w^{*} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2} - \| w^{*} - w_{k + 1}^{s}\|^{2}} \right\rbrack,} \\ & {= F(w^{*}),} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$
(4.54)

second equality follows from, $\mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {\widetilde{\nabla}}_{s,k} \right\rbrack = \nabla f(w_{k}^{s}) + \frac{m - 1}{m}\nabla f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1})$, first inequality follows from Lemma 1, second inequality follows from the convexity, i.e., f(w*) ≥ f(wks) +  < ∇f(wks), w* − wks> and Young's inequality, i.e., xTy ≤ 1/(2δ)∥x∥2 + δ/2∥y∥2 for δ > 0, and last equality follows by choosing $\delta = \frac{mL\beta}{(m - 1)}$.


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} & {\text{And},\,\mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {< \nabla f(w_{k}^{s}) - {\widetilde{\nabla}}_{s,k},w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s} > - \frac{L(\beta - 1)}{2}\| w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2}} \right\rbrack} \\ & {\leq \mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {\frac{1}{2L(\beta - 1)}\|\nabla f(w_{k}^{s}) - {\widetilde{\nabla}}_{s,k}\|^{2} + \frac{L(\beta - 1)}{2}\| w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2} - \frac{L(\beta - 1)}{2}\| w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2}} \right\rbrack} \\ & {= \frac{1}{2L(\beta - 1)}\mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {\|{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{s,k} - \nabla f(w_{k}^{s})\|^{2}} \right\rbrack} \\ & {\leq \frac{1}{2L(\beta - 1)}\left\lbrack {8L\alpha(b)\left\lbrack {F(w_{k}^{s}) - F^{*}} \right\rbrack + \frac{8L\left( {\alpha(b)m^{2} + {(m - 1)}^{2}} \right)}{m^{2}}\left\lbrack {F({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - F^{*}} \right\rbrack + R^{{}^{\prime}}} \right\rbrack} \\ & {= \frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)}\left\lbrack {F(w_{k}^{s}) - F^{*}} \right\rbrack + \frac{4\left( {\alpha(b)m^{2} + {(m - 1)}^{2}} \right)}{m^{2}(\beta - 1)}\left\lbrack {F({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - F^{*}} \right\rbrack + R^{{}^{\prime\prime}}} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$
(4.55)

first inequality follows from Young's inequality and second inequality follows from Lemma 7 and R′′ = R′/(2L(β − 1)). Now, substituting the values into 4.53 from inequalities 4.54 and 4.55, and taking expectation w.r.t. mini-batches, we have


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} & {\mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {F(w_{k + 1}^{s})} \right\rbrack \leq F(w^{*}) + \frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)}\left\lbrack {F(w_{k}^{s}) - F^{*}} \right\rbrack} \\ & {\quad + \frac{4\left( {\alpha(b)m^{2} + {(m - 1)}^{2}} \right)}{m^{2}(\beta - 1)}\left\lbrack {F({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - F^{*}} \right\rbrack + R^{{}^{\prime\prime}}} \\ & {\mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {F(w_{k + 1}^{s}) - F(w^{*})} \right\rbrack \leq \frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)}\left\lbrack {F(w_{k}^{s}) - F^{*}} \right\rbrack} \\ & {\quad + \frac{4\left( {\alpha(b)m^{2} + {(m - 1)}^{2}} \right)}{m^{2}(\beta - 1)}\left\lbrack {F({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - F^{*}} \right\rbrack + R^{{}^{\prime\prime}}} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$
(4.56)

Taking sum over k = 0, 1, ..., (m − 1) and dividing by m, we have


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} & {\frac{1}{m}{\sum\limits_{k = 0}^{m - 1}\mathbb{E}}\left\lbrack {F(w_{k + 1}^{s}) - F(w^{*})} \right\rbrack} \\ & {\leq \frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)}\frac{1}{m}{\sum\limits_{k = 0}^{m - 1}\left\lbrack {F(w_{k}^{s}) - F(w^{*})} \right\rbrack} + \frac{4\left( {\alpha(b)m^{2} + {(m - 1)}^{2}} \right)}{m^{2}(\beta - 1)}\left\lbrack {F({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - F(w^{*})} \right\rbrack + R^{{}^{\prime\prime}},} \\ & {\frac{1}{m}{\sum\limits_{k = 1}^{m}\mathbb{E}}\left\lbrack {F(w_{k}^{s}) - F(w^{*})} \right\rbrack} \\ & {\leq \frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)}\frac{1}{m}\left\{ {{\sum\limits_{k = 1}^{m}\left\lbrack {F(w_{k}^{s}) - F(w^{*})} \right\rbrack} + F(w_{0}^{s}) - F(w^{*}) - \left\{ F(w_{m}^{s}) - F(w^{*}) \right\}} \right\}} \\ & {\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\, + \frac{4\left( {\alpha(b)m^{2} + {(m - 1)}^{2}} \right)}{m^{2}(\beta - 1)}\left\lbrack {F({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - F(w^{*})} \right\rbrack + R^{{}^{\prime\prime}}} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$
(4.57)

Subtracting $\frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)}\frac{1}{m}{\sum\limits_{k = 1}^{m}\left\lbrack {F(w_{k}^{s}) - F(w^{*})} \right\rbrack}$ from both sides, we have


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} & {\left( {1 - \frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)}} \right)\frac{1}{m}{\sum\limits_{k = 1}^{m}\mathbb{E}}\left\lbrack {F(w_{k}^{s}) - F(w^{*})} \right\rbrack} \\ & {\leq \frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)}\frac{1}{m}\left\lbrack {F(w_{0}^{s}) - F(w^{*}) - \left\{ F(w_{m}^{s}) - F(w^{*}) \right\}} \right\rbrack} \\ & {\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\, + \frac{4\left( {\alpha(b)m^{2} + {(m - 1)}^{2}} \right)}{m^{2}(\beta - 1)}\left\lbrack {F({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - F(w^{*})} \right\rbrack + R^{{}^{\prime\prime}}} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$
(4.58)

Since F(wms) − F(w*) ≥ 0 so dropping this term and using Assumption 3, we have


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} & {\left( {1 - \frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)}} \right)\frac{1}{m}{\sum\limits_{k = 1}^{m}\mathbb{E}}\left\lbrack {F(w_{k}^{s}) - F(w^{*})} \right\rbrack} \\ & {\leq \frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)}\frac{1}{m}\left\lbrack {F(w_{0}^{s}) - F(w^{*})} \right\rbrack + \frac{4\left( {\alpha(b)m^{2} + {(m - 1)}^{2}} \right)}{m^{2}(\beta - 1)}\left\lbrack {F({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - F(w^{*})} \right\rbrack + R^{{}^{\prime\prime}}} \\ & {\leq \frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)}\frac{1}{m}\left\lbrack {c\left( {F({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - F(w^{*})} \right)} \right\rbrack + \frac{4\left( {\alpha(b)m^{2} + {(m - 1)}^{2}} \right)}{m^{2}(\beta - 1)}\left\lbrack {F({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - F(w^{*})} \right\rbrack + R^{{}^{\prime\prime}}} \\ & {= \left( {\frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)}\frac{c}{m} + \frac{4\left( {\alpha(b)m^{2} + {(m - 1)}^{2}} \right)}{m^{2}(\beta - 1)}} \right)\left\lbrack \left( {F({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - F(w^{*})} \right) \right\rbrack + R^{{}^{\prime\prime}},} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$
(4.59)

Dividing both sides by $\left( {1 - \frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)}} \right)$, and since ${\widetilde{w}}^{s} = 1/m{\sum\limits_{k = 1}^{m}w_{k}^{s}}$ so by convexity, $F({\widetilde{w}}^{s}) \leq 1/m{\sum\limits_{k = 1}^{m}F}(w_{k}^{s})$, we have


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} & {\mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {F({\widetilde{w}}^{s}) - F^{*}} \right\rbrack \leq \left( {\frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1 - 4\alpha(b))}\frac{c}{m} + \frac{4\left( {\alpha(b)m^{2} + {(m - 1)}^{2}} \right)}{m^{2}(\beta - 1 - 4\alpha(b))}} \right)\left\lbrack {F({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - F(w^{*})} \right\rbrack + R^{{}^{\prime\prime\prime}},} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$
(4.60)

where $R^{{}^{\prime\prime\prime}} = R^{{}^{\prime\prime}}\left( {1 - \frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)}} \right)^{- 1}$. Now, applying above inequality recursively, we have


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{l} {\mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {F({\widetilde{w}}^{s}) - F(w^{*})} \right\rbrack \leq C^{s}\left\lbrack {F({\widetilde{w}}^{0}) - F(w^{*})} \right\rbrack + R^{{}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}},} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$
(4.61)

inequality follows for R′′′′ = R′′′/(1 − C), since ${\sum\limits_{i = 0}^{k}r^{i}} \leq {\sum\limits_{i = 0}^{\infty}r^{i}} = \frac{1}{1 - r},\quad\| r\| < 1$ and $C = \left( {\frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1 - 4\alpha(b))}\frac{c}{m} + \frac{4\left( {\alpha(b)m^{2} + {(m - 1)}^{2}} \right)}{m^{2}(\beta - 1 - 4\alpha(b))}} \right)$. For certain choice of β, one can easily prove that C < 1. This proves linear convergence with some initial error.

Theorem 5 Under the assumptions of Lipschitz continuity and strong convexity with non-smooth regularizer, the convergence of SAAG-IV is given below:


$$\begin{array}{l} {\mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {F({\widetilde{w}}^{s}) - F^{*}} \right\rbrack \leq C^{s}\left\lbrack {F({\widetilde{w}}^{0}) - F^{*}} \right\rbrack + V,} \\ \end{array}$$
(4.62)

where, $C = \left( {\frac{Lc\beta}{m\mu} + \frac{4c\alpha(b)}{m(\beta - 1)} - \frac{c(m - 1)}{m^{2}} + \frac{4\left( {\alpha(b)m^{2} + {(m - 1)}^{2}} \right)}{m^{2}(\beta - 1)}} \right)\text{\textbackslash\textbackslash}\left( {1 - \frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)} - \frac{c(m - 1)}{m^{2}} + \frac{4c\alpha(b)}{m(\beta - 1)}} \right)^{- 1} < 1$ and V is constant.

Proof 4.8 By smoothness, we have,


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} {f(w_{k + 1}^{s})} & {\leq f(w_{k}^{s}) + < \nabla f(w_{k}^{s}),w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s} > + \frac{L}{2}\| w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2}} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$

Now,


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} & {F(w_{k + 1}^{s}) = f(w_{k + 1}^{s}) + g(w_{k + 1}^{s})} \\ & {\leq f(w_{k}^{s}) + g(w_{k + 1}^{s}) + < \nabla f(w_{k}^{s}),w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s} > + \frac{L}{2}\| w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2}} \\ & {= f(w_{k}^{s}) + g(w_{k + 1}^{s}) + < \nabla f(w_{k}^{s}),w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s} > + \frac{L\beta}{2}\| w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2} - \frac{L(\beta - 1}{2}\| w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2}} \\ & {= f(w_{k}^{s}) + g(w_{k + 1}^{s}) + < {\widetilde{\nabla}}_{s,k},w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s} > + \frac{L\beta}{2}\| w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2} +} \\ & {\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\, < \nabla f(w_{k}^{s}) - {\widetilde{\nabla}}_{s,k},w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s} > - \frac{L(\beta - 1}{2}\| w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2}} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$
(4.63)

where β is appropriately chosen positive value. Now, separately simplifying the terms, we have

second equality follows from, $\mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {\widetilde{\nabla}}_{s,k} \right\rbrack = \nabla f(w_{k}^{s}) + \frac{m - 1}{m}\nabla f({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1})$, first inequality follows from Lemma 1 and second inequality follows from the convexity, i.e., f(x) ≥ f(y) +  < ∇f(y), x − y>.


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} & {\text{And},\,\mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {< \nabla f(w_{k}^{s}) - {\widetilde{\nabla}}_{s,k},w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s} > - \frac{L(\beta - 1)}{2}\| w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2}} \right\rbrack} \\ & {\leq \mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {\frac{1}{2L(\beta - 1)}\|\nabla f(w_{k}^{s}) - {\widetilde{\nabla}}_{s,k}\|^{2} + \frac{L(\beta - 1)}{2}\| w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2} - \frac{L(\beta - 1)}{2}\| w_{k + 1}^{s} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2}} \right\rbrack} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} & {= \frac{1}{2L(\beta - 1)}\mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {\|{\widetilde{\nabla}}_{s,k} - \nabla f(w_{k}^{s})\|^{2}} \right\rbrack} \\ & {\leq \frac{1}{2L(\beta - 1)}\left\lbrack {8L\alpha(b)\left\lbrack {F(w_{k}^{s}) - F^{*}} \right\rbrack + \frac{8L\left( {\alpha(b)m^{2} + {(m - 1)}^{2}} \right)}{m^{2}}\left\lbrack {F({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - F^{*}} \right\rbrack + R^{{}^{\prime}}} \right\rbrack} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} & {= \frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)}\left\lbrack {F(w_{k}^{s}) - F^{*}} \right\rbrack + \frac{4\left( {\alpha(b)m^{2} + {(m - 1)}^{2}} \right)}{m^{2}(\beta - 1)}\left\lbrack {F({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - F^{*}} \right\rbrack + R^{{}^{\prime\prime}},} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$
(4.65)

first inequality follows from Young's inequality and second inequality follows from Lemma 7 and R′′ = R′/(2L(β − 1)). Now, substituting the values into 4.63 from inequalities 4.64 and 4.65, and taking expectation w.r.t. mini-batches, we have


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} & {\mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {F(w_{k + 1}^{s})} \right\rbrack} \\ & {\leq F(w^{*}) + \frac{L\beta}{2}\left\lbrack {\| w^{*} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2} - \| w^{*} - w_{k + 1}^{s}\|^{2}} \right\rbrack + \frac{m - 1}{m}\left\lbrack {f(w_{k + 1}^{s}) - f(w_{k}^{s})} \right\rbrack} \\ & {\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\, + \frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)}\left\lbrack {F(w_{k}^{s}) - F^{*}} \right\rbrack + \frac{4\left( {\alpha(b)m^{2} + {(m - 1)}^{2}} \right)}{m^{2}(\beta - 1)}\left\lbrack {F({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - F^{*}} \right\rbrack + R^{{}^{\prime\prime}}} \\ & {\mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {F(w_{k + 1}^{s}) - F(w^{*})} \right\rbrack} \\ & {\leq \frac{L\beta}{2}\left\lbrack {\| w^{*} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2} - \| w^{*} - w_{k + 1}^{s}\|^{2}} \right\rbrack + \frac{m - 1}{m}\left\lbrack {f(w_{k + 1}^{s}) - f(w_{k}^{s})} \right\rbrack} \\ & {\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\, + \frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)}\left\lbrack {F(w_{k}^{s}) - F^{*}} \right\rbrack + \frac{4\left( {\alpha(b)m^{2} + {(m - 1)}^{2}} \right)}{m^{2}(\beta - 1)}\left\lbrack {F({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - F^{*}} \right\rbrack + R^{{}^{\prime\prime}}} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$
(4.66)

Taking sum over k = 0, 1, ..., (m − 1) and dividing by m, we have


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} & {\frac{1}{m}{\sum\limits_{k = 0}^{m - 1}\mathbb{E}}\left\lbrack {F(w_{k + 1}^{s}) - F(w^{*})} \right\rbrack} \\ & {\leq \frac{1}{m}{\sum\limits_{k = 0}^{m - 1}\left\{ {\frac{L\beta}{2}\left\lbrack {\| w^{*} - w_{k}^{s}\|^{2} - \| w^{*} - w_{k + 1}^{s}\|^{2}} \right\rbrack + \frac{m - 1}{m}\left\lbrack {f(w_{k + 1}^{s}) - f(w_{k}^{s})} \right\rbrack} \right\}}} \\ & {\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\, + \frac{1}{m}{\sum\limits_{k = 0}^{m - 1}\left\{ {\frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)}\left\lbrack {F(w_{k}^{s}) - F^{*}} \right\rbrack + \frac{4\left( {\alpha(b)m^{2} + {(m - 1)}^{2}} \right)}{m^{2}(\beta - 1)}\left\lbrack {F({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - F^{*}} \right\rbrack + R^{{}^{\prime\prime}}} \right\}},} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} & {\frac{1}{m}{\sum\limits_{k = 1}^{m}\mathbb{E}}\left\lbrack {F(w_{k}^{s}) - F(w^{*})} \right\rbrack} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} & {\leq \frac{L\beta}{2m}\left\lbrack {\| w^{*} - w_{0}^{s}\|^{2} - \| w^{*} - w_{m}^{s}\|^{2}} \right\rbrack + \frac{m - 1}{m^{2}}\left\lbrack {f(w_{m}^{s}) - f(w_{0}^{s})} \right\rbrack} \\ & {\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\, + \frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)}\frac{1}{m}\left\{ {{\sum\limits_{k = 1}^{m}\left\lbrack {F(w_{k}^{s}) - F(w^{*})} \right\rbrack} + F(w_{0}^{s}) - F(w^{*}) - \left( {F(w_{m}^{s}) - F(w^{*})} \right)} \right\}} \\ & {\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\, + \frac{4\left( {\alpha(b)m^{2} + {(m - 1)}^{2}} \right)}{m^{2}(\beta - 1)}\left\lbrack {F({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - F(w^{*})} \right\rbrack + R^{{}^{\prime\prime}}} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$
(4.67)

Subtracting, $\frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)}\frac{1}{m}{\sum\limits_{k = 1}^{m}\left\lbrack {F(w_{k}^{s}) - F(w^{*})} \right\rbrack}$ from both sides, we have


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} {\lbrack ll\rbrack} & {\left( {1 - \frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)}} \right)\frac{1}{m}{\sum\limits_{k = 1}^{m}\mathbb{E}}\left\lbrack {F(w_{k}^{s}) - F(w^{*})} \right\rbrack} \\ & {\leq \frac{L\beta}{2m}\left\lbrack {\| w^{*} - w_{0}^{s}\|^{2} - \| w^{*} - w_{m}^{s}\|^{2}} \right\rbrack + \frac{m - 1}{m^{2}}\left\lbrack {f(w_{m}^{s}) - f(w_{0}^{s})} \right\rbrack} \\ & {\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\, + \frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)}\frac{1}{m}\left\{ {F(w_{0}^{s}) - F(w^{*}) - \left( {F(w_{m}^{s}) - F(w^{*})} \right)} \right\}} \\ & {\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\, + \frac{4\left( {\alpha(b)m^{2} + {(m - 1)}^{2}} \right)}{m^{2}(\beta - 1)}\left\lbrack {F({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - F(w^{*})} \right\rbrack + R^{{}^{\prime\prime}}} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} & {\leq \frac{L\beta}{2m}\left\lbrack {\| w^{*} - w_{0}^{s}\|^{2}} \right\rbrack + \frac{m - 1}{m^{2}}\left\lbrack {F(w_{m}^{s}) - F(w_{0}^{s})} \right\rbrack} \\ & {\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\, + \frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)}\frac{1}{m}\left\{ {F(w_{0}^{s}) - F(w^{*}) - \left( {F(w_{m}^{s}) - F(w^{*})} \right)} \right\}} \\ & {\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\, + \frac{4\left( {\alpha(b)m^{2} + {(m - 1)}^{2}} \right)}{m^{2}(\beta - 1)}\left\lbrack {F({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - F(w^{*})} \right\rbrack + R^{{}^{\prime\prime\prime}}} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} & {\leq \frac{L\beta}{2m}\frac{2}{\mu}\left\lbrack {F(w_{0}^{s}) - F(w^{*})} \right\rbrack + \frac{m - 1}{m^{2}}\left\lbrack {F(w_{m}^{s}) - F(w_{0}^{s})} \right\rbrack} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} & {\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\, + \frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)}\frac{1}{m}\left\{ {F(w_{0}^{s}) - F(w^{*}) - \left( {F(w_{m}^{s}) - F(w^{*})} \right)} \right\}} \\ & {\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\, + \frac{4\left( {\alpha(b)m^{2} + {(m - 1)}^{2}} \right)}{m^{2}(\beta - 1)}\left\lbrack {F({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - F(w^{*})} \right\rbrack + R^{{}^{\prime\prime\prime}}} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} & {= \left( {\frac{L\beta}{m\mu} + \frac{4\alpha(b)}{m(\beta - 1)} - \frac{m - 1}{m^{2}}} \right)\left\lbrack {F(w_{0}^{s}) - F(w^{*})} \right\rbrack + \left( {\frac{m - 1}{m^{2}} - \frac{4\alpha(b)}{m(\beta - 1)}} \right)} \\ & {\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\, \times \,\left\lbrack {F(w_{m}^{s}) - F(w^{*})} \right\rbrack + \frac{4\left( {\alpha(b)m^{2} + {(m - 1)}^{2}} \right)}{m^{2}(\beta - 1)}\left\lbrack {F({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - F(w^{*})} \right\rbrack + R^{{}^{\prime\prime\prime}}} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} & {\leq \left( {\frac{L\beta}{m\mu} + \frac{4\alpha(b)}{m(\beta - 1)} - \frac{m - 1}{m^{2}}} \right)c\left\lbrack {F({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - F(w^{*})} \right\rbrack} \\ & {\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\, + \left( {\frac{m - 1}{m^{2}} - \frac{4\alpha(b)}{m(\beta - 1)}} \right)c\left\lbrack {F({\widetilde{w}}^{s}) - F(w^{*})} \right\rbrack} \\ & {\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\, + \frac{4\left( {\alpha(b)m^{2} + {(m - 1)}^{2}} \right)}{m^{2}(\beta - 1)}\left\lbrack {F({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - F(w^{*})} \right\rbrack + R^{{}^{\prime\prime\prime}}} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} & {\leq \left( {\frac{Lc\beta}{m\mu} + \frac{4c\alpha(b)}{m(\beta - 1)} - \frac{c(m - 1)}{m^{2}} + \frac{4\left( {\alpha(b)m^{2} + {(m - 1)}^{2}} \right)}{m^{2}(\beta - 1)}} \right)\left\lbrack {F({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - F(w^{*})} \right\rbrack} \\ & {\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\, + \left( {\frac{m - 1}{m^{2}} - \frac{4\alpha(b)}{m(\beta - 1)}} \right)c\left\lbrack {F({\widetilde{w}}^{s}) - F(w^{*})} \right\rbrack + R^{{}^{\prime\prime\prime}}} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$
(4.68)

second inequality follows from dropping, ∥w* − wms∥2 ≥ 0 and converting, f(wms) − f(w0s) to F(wms) − F(w0s) by introducing some constant, third inequality follows from the strong convexity, i.e., ∥w0s − w*∥2 ≤ 2/μ(f(w0s)−f*), fourth inequality follows from Assumption 3 and R′′′ = R′′ + (m − 1)g(w0s)/m2. Since ${\widetilde{w}}^{s} = 1/m{\sum\limits_{k = 1}^{m}w_{k}^{s}}$ so by convexity using, $f({\widetilde{w}}^{s}) \leq 1/m{\sum\limits_{k = 1}^{m}f}(w_{k}^{s})$, and subtracting $\left( {\frac{m - 1}{m^{2}} - \frac{4\alpha(b)}{m(\beta - 1)}} \right)c\left\lbrack {F({\widetilde{w}}^{s}) - F(w^{*})} \right\rbrack$ from both sides, we have


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} & {\left( {1 - \frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)} - \frac{c(m - 1)}{m^{2}} + \frac{4c\alpha(b)}{m(\beta - 1)}} \right)\mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {F({\widetilde{w}}^{s}) - F(w^{*})} \right\rbrack} \\ & {\leq \left( {\frac{Lc\beta}{m\mu} + \frac{4c\alpha(b)}{m(\beta - 1)} - \frac{c(m - 1)}{m^{2}} + \frac{4\left( {\alpha(b)m^{2} + {(m - 1)}^{2}} \right)}{m^{2}(\beta - 1)}} \right)\left\lbrack {F({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - F(w^{*})} \right\rbrack + R^{{}^{\prime\prime\prime}}} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$
(4.69)

Dividing both sides by $\left( {1 - \frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)} - \frac{c(m - 1)}{m^{2}} + \frac{4c\alpha(b)}{m(\beta - 1)}} \right)$, we have


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{ll} & {\mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {F({\widetilde{w}}^{s}) - F(w^{*})} \right\rbrack \leq C\left\lbrack {F({\widetilde{w}}^{s - 1}) - F(w^{*})} \right\rbrack + R^{{}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}}} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$
(4.70)

where $C = \left( {\frac{Lc\beta}{m\mu} + \frac{4c\alpha(b)}{m(\beta - 1)} - \frac{c(m - 1)}{m^{2}} + \frac{4\left( {\alpha(b)m^{2} + {(m - 1)}^{2}} \right)}{m^{2}(\beta - 1)}} \right).\text{\textbackslash\textbackslash}\left( {1 - \frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)} - \frac{c(m - 1)}{m^{2}} + \frac{4c\alpha(b)}{m(\beta - 1)}} \right)^{- 1}$ and $R^{{}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}} = R^{{}^{\prime\prime\prime}}\left( {1 - \frac{4\alpha(b)}{(\beta - 1)} - \frac{c(m - 1)}{m^{2}} + \frac{4c\alpha(b)}{m(\beta - 1)}} \right)^{- 1}$. Now, applying this inequality recursively, we have


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{l} {\mathbb{E}\left\lbrack {F({\widetilde{w}}^{s}) - F(w^{*})} \right\rbrack \leq C^{s}\left\lbrack {F({\widetilde{w}}^{0}) - F(w^{*})} \right\rbrack + R^{{}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime\prime}},} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$
(4.71)

inequality follows for R′′′′′ = R′′′′/(1 − C), since ${\sum\limits_{i = 0}^{k}r^{i}} \leq {\sum\limits_{i = 0}^{\infty}r^{i}} = \frac{1}{1 - r},\quad\| r\| < 1$. For certain choice of β, one can easily prove that C < 1. This proves linear convergence with some initial error.

All the proofs given here prove linear convergence (as per definition of convergence) of SAAG-IV for all the four combinations of smoothness and strong-convexity with some initial errors due to the constant terms in the results. SAAGs are based on intuitions from practice [23] and they try to give more importance to the latest gradient values than the older gradient values, which make them biased techniques and results into this extra constant term. This constant term signifies that SAAGs converge to a region close to the solution, which is very practical because all the machine learning algorithms are used to solve the problems approximately and we never find an exact solution for the problem [15], because of computational difficulty. Moreover, the constant term pops up due to the mini-batched gradient value at optimal point, i.e., $\frac{1}{\left| B_{k} \right|}{\sum\limits_{i \in B_{k}}\nabla}f_{i}(w^{*})$. If the size of the mini-batch increases and eventually becomes equal to the dataset then this constant becomes equal to full gradient and vanishes, i.e., $\frac{1}{n}{\sum\limits_{i = 1}^{n}\nabla}f_{i}(w^{*}) = 0.$

The linear convergence for all combinations of strong convexity and smoothness of the regularizer, is the maximum rate exhibited by the first order methods without curvature information. SAG, SVRG, SAGA and VR-SGD also exhibit linear convergence for the strong convexity and smooth problem but except VR-SGD, they don't cover all the cases, e.g., SVRG does not cover the non-strongly convex cases. However, the theoretical results provided by VR-SGD, prove linear convergence for strongly convex cases, like our results, but VR-SGD provides only O(1/T) convergence for non-strongly convex cases, unlike our linear convergence results.

4.6  Experimental Results

In this section, we have presented the experimental results5

4.6.1  Experimental Setup

The experiments are reported using six different criteria which plot suboptimality (objective – best value) versus epochs (where one epoch refers to one pass through the dataset), suboptimality versus gradients /n, suboptimality versus time, accuracy versus time, accuracy versus epochs and accuracy versus gradients /n. The x-axis and y-axis data are represented in linear and log scale, respectively. The experiments use the following binary datasets: rcv1, news20, real-sim and Adult (also called as a9a), whose details are given in the Table 1.1. All the datasets are divided into 80% and 20% as training and test dataset, respectively. The value of regularization parameter is set as λ = 1 * 10 − 5(including λ1, λ2) for all the algorithms, unless specified. The parameters for, Stochastic Backtracking-Armijo line Search (SBAS), are set as: α = 0.1, β = 0.5 and learning rate is initialized as, η = 1.0. The inner iterations are set as, m = n/b (as used in [39]). Moreover, in SBAS, algorithms looks for maximum 10 iterations and after that it returns the current value of learning rate if it reduces the objective value otherwise it returns 0.0. This is done to avoid sticking in the algorithm because of stochastic line search. All the experiments have been conducted on MacBook Air (8 GB 1600 MHz DDR3, 1.6 GHz Intel Core i5 and 256GB SSD) using MEX files.

4.6.2  Results with Smooth Problem

The results are reported with l2-regularized logistic regression problem as given below:


$$\begin{array}{l} {\underset{w}{\min}\, F(w) = \frac{1}{n}{\sum\limits_{i = 1}^{n}{\log}}\left( {1 + \exp\left( {- y_{i}w^{T}x_{i}} \right)} \right) + \frac{\lambda}{2}\| w\|^{2}.} \\ \end{array}$$
(4.72)

Figure 4.1 reports results with news20 dataset. SAAG-III and IV perform better than SVRG and VR-SGD, but SAAG-IV gives best results. This is because as the mini-batch size or the dataset size increases, SAAG-II and SAAG-IV perform better (as reported in [23]).

4.6.3  Results with Non-smooth Problem

The results are reported with elastic-net-regularized logistic regression problem (non-smooth regularizer) as given below:


$$\begin{array}{l} {\underset{w}{\min}\, F(w) = \frac{1}{n}{\sum\limits_{i = 1}^{n}{\log}}\left( {1 + \exp\left( {- y_{i}w^{T}x_{i}} \right)} \right) + \frac{\lambda_{1}}{2}\| w\|^{2} + \lambda_{2}\| w\|_{1},} \\ \end{array}$$
(4.73)

where $f(w) = \frac{1}{n}{\sum\limits_{i = 1}^{n}{\log}}\left( {1 + \exp\left( {- y_{i}w^{T}x_{i}} \right)} \right) + \frac{\lambda_{1}}{2}\| w\|^{2}$ and g(w) = λ2∥w∥1.

Figure 4.2 represents the comparative study of SAAG-III, IV, SVRG and VR-SGD on rcv1 dataset. As it is clear from the figure, for all the six criteria plots, SAAG-III and IV outperform SVRG and VR-SGD, SAAG-IV giving best results in terms of suboptimality but in terms of accuracy, SAAG-III and IV have close performance except for accuracy versus gradients /n, where SAAG-III gives better results because SAAG-III calculates gradients at last iterate only unlike SAAG-IV which calculates gradients at snap point and last iterate.

4.6.4  Mini-batch Block-coordinate versus Mini-batch setting

This subsection provides empirical evidence for the observation that mini-batch block-coordinate setting (BBOF), i.e., using stochastic approximation and coordinate together does not work well. Figure 4.3 compares three methods, namely, SAG, SAAG-I and SAAG-II without sampling features, i.e., using all features and using a block of 1000 features, and mini-batch of 1000 data points. Figure plots suboptimality against training time on rcv1 and real-sim datasets and uses stochastic backtracking line search method to find the learning rate. It is clear from the figure that doubly sampled methods, i.e., methods using BBOF setting lag far behind the singly sampled methods, i.e., methods using only data sampling and all features. Thus, it is not suitable to use stochastic approximation and coordinate descent approaches together because the advantage of having low per-iteration complexity is lost in the implementation overhead due to double sampling. But if we could find ways to reduce the implementation overhead then BBOF could be very effective to solve big data problems.

4.6.5  Results with SVM

This subsection compares SAAGs against SVRG and VR-SGD on SVM problem with mushroom and gisette datasets. Methods use stochastic backtracking line search method to find the step size. Figure 4.4 presents the results and compares the suboptimality against the training time (in seconds). Results are similar to experiments with logistic regression but are not that smooth. SAAGs outperform other methods on mushroom dataset (first row) and gisette dataset (second row) for suboptimality against training time and accuracy against time but all methods give almost similar results on accuracy versus training time for mushroom dataset. SAAG-IV outperforms other method and SAAG-III sometimes lags behind VR-SGD method. It is also observed that results with logistic regression are better than the results with the SVM problem. The optimization problem for SVM is given below:


$$\begin{array}{l} {\underset{w}{\min}\, F(w) = \frac{1}{n}{\sum\limits_{i = 1}^{n}{\max}}\left( {0,1 - y_{i}w^{T}x_{i}} \right)^{2} + \frac{\lambda}{2}\| w\|^{2},} \\ \end{array}$$
(4.74)

where λ is the regularization coefficient (also penalty parameter) which balances the trade-off between margin size and error [25].

4.7  Conclusion

We have proposed novel variants of SAAG-I and II, called SAAG-III and IV, respectively, by using average of iterates for SAAG-III as a starting point, and average of iterates and last iterate for SAAG-IV as the snap point and starting point, respectively, for new epoch, except the first one. SAAGs (I, II, III and IV) are also extended to solve non-smooth problems by using two different update rules and introducing proximal step for non-smooth problem. Theoretical results proved linear convergence of SAAG-IV for all the four combinations of smoothness and strong-convexity with some initial errors, in expectation. The empirical results proved the efficacy of proposed methods against existing variance reduction methods in terms of, accuracy and suboptimality, against training time, epochs and gradients /n.

Chapter 5 Learning and Data Access

DOI: 10.1201/9781003240167-5

The training time of models has two major components time to access the data and time to process (learn from) the data. So far, the research has focused only on the second part, i.e., learning from the data. In this chapter, we have proposed one possible solution to handle the big data problems in machine learning. The idea is to reduce the training time through reducing data access time by proposing systematic sampling and cyclic/sequential sampling to select mini-batches from the dataset. To prove the effectiveness of proposed sampling techniques, we have used empirical risk minimization, which is commonly used machine learning problem, for strongly convex and smooth case. The problem has been solved using SAG, SAGA, SVRG, SAAG-II and MBSGD (Mini-batched SGD), each using two step determination techniques, namely, constant step size and backtracking line search method. Theoretical results prove similar convergence for systematic and cyclic sampling as the widely used random sampling technique, in expectation. Empirical results with benchmarked datasets prove the efficacy of the proposed sampling techniques and show up to six times faster training.

5.1 Introduction

Nowadays, the major challenge is to develop efficient and scalable learning algorithms for dealing with big data problems, as discussed in detail in Section 3.1. The training time of models have two major components [144] as:


$$\begin{array}{l} {\text{training~time} = \text{time~to~access~data} + \text{time~to~process~data}.} \\ \end{array}$$
(5.1)

As it is known that for processing any data or running any program, it should be first brought into memory, more precisely into RAM (Random Access Memory), from hard disk. The time taken in bringing the data or program from hard disk into memory is called access time. Access time has, further, three components, namely, seek time (it is the time taken by the reading head to move from current position up to the track containing the data), rotational latency time (it is the time taken by the reading head from the current position to reach up to the sector/block containing the data) and transfer time (it is the time taken to transfer data from sector/block over the disk to memory). Moreover, data is not read content wise rather block-wise, where each block can have multiple sectors. Now, it is interesting to note that for data stored on the contiguous memory or in the close proximity, has lesser data access time as compared with the data dispersed far away from each other. This is due to lesser seek, latency and transfer times. In case of SSD (Solid State Disk) and RAM (Random Access Memory), there are no seek and latency times because they do not have moving parts and they are based on direct access mechanism, but transfer time still plays its role. Since data are read/written block-wise so for contiguous data access, there would be one or two transfer times but dispersed data access would require more number of transfer times. Moreover, cache memory strategies also favor the contiguous memory access and make it faster as compared to dispersed data access. Thus, contiguous data access time is faster than dispersed data access, in all the cases whether data is stored on RAM, SSD or HDD. But the difference in access time would be more prominent for HDD.

The second component of training time is processing (learning) time which is the time taken by the CPU (Central Processing Unit) to process the data to solve for model parameters. Due to mini-batching and iterative learning algorithms, the accessing and processing of data is intermixed and occurs quite frequently. The data access time is dependent on the sampling technique and processing time is dependent on the method used to solve the problem. Till this date, to improve the training time, most of the focus is given only to improve the processing time by using different methods. The training time is dependent on both data accessing time and data processing time, and this is to be noted that, generally, data access time is very high as compared to the data processing time. Thus, efforts should be equally done to improve the data accessing time. This chapter has tried to reduce the training time for big data problems by reducing the data access time using systematic sampling technique [93, 94] and cyclic sampling to select mini-batches of data points from the dataset.

5.1.1  Optimization Problem

Empirical risk minimization (ERM) problem [111] is a commonly used problem in machine learning and statistics, which typically consists of a loss function and a regularization term. In this chapter, l2-regularized ERM problem with the assumptions of strong convexity and smoothness, is used for demonstrating the effectiveness of proposed sampling techniques. For the training data {(x1,y1),(x2,y2),...,(xl,yl)}, where xi ∈ ℝn, yi ∈ ℝ, and for loss functions f1,f2,...,fl: ℝn→ℝ, model parameter vector w ∈ ℝn and regularization coefficient C > 0, l2-regularized ERM problem is given by


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{l} {\underset{w}{\min}\,\, f(w) = \frac{1}{l}{\sum\limits_{i = 1}^{l}f_{i}}(w) + \frac{C}{2}\| w\|^{2},} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$
(5.2)

where first term is the data fitting term and second term is used for avoiding the over-fitting of data. Commonly used loss functions are the square loss (wTxi−yi)2, the logistic loss log (1+exp(−yiwTxi)) and the hinge loss max (0,1−yiwTxi). In this chapter, experimentation uses logistic loss function. When problem represented by eq.(5.2) is a large-scale or big data problem then per iteration complexity of learning algorithms with traditional methods like Gradient Descent is O(nl) which is very high. This is because, such problems have large number of data points (l) or large number of features (n), or both, and each iteration updates n variables over l data points. Because of this high computational complexity per iteration it would be very expensive or even infeasible for single machine to process a single iteration of learning algorithm. Stochastic approximation approach is widely used for handling such cases (e.g., [148, 63, 35, 19, 115, 23]), which uses one data point or mini-batch of data points during each iteration. The reduced subproblem with mini-batch Bj of data points for jth inner iteration is given by


$$\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{l} {\underset{w}{\min}\,\,\frac{1}{\left| B_{j} \right|}{\sum\limits_{i \in B_{j}}f_{i}}(w) + \frac{C}{2}\| w\|^{2},\quad j = 1,2,...,m.} \\ \end{array} \\ \end{array}$$
(5.3)

The iteration complexity for solving this reduced subproblem is O(n) (for one data point) or O(n|Bj|) (for mini-batch of data points) where |Bj| is the size of mini-batch, which is very low and independent of l. Since it is easier to solve the problem (5.3) and this is widely used approach to handle large-scale problems so the chapter uses this reduced subproblem approach.

5.1.2  Literature Review

Big data challenge is one of the major challenge in machine learning and stochastic approximation is one of the important approach to deal with large-scale learning problems, as discussed in detail in Chapter 3. Sampling plays an important role in the convergence and training time of learning algorithms. For selecting one data point or mini-batch of data points from the dataset in stochastic approximation approach, random sampling (e.g., [19, 35, 63, 115, 148]) is widely used, and other sampling techniques are importance sampling (e.g., [34, 151]), stratified sampling [150] and adaptive sampling [48]. Importance sampling is a non-uniform sampling technique, which uses properties of data for finding probabilities of selection of data points in the iterative process, leading to the acceleration of training process. Importance sampling involves some overhead in calculating the probabilities and the average computational complexity per iteration of importance sampling could be more than the computational complexity of the iteration in some cases, like, when it is implemented dynamically. Stratified sampling technique divides the dataset into clusters of similar data points and then mini-batch of data points are selected from the clusters. In adaptive sampling [48], information about the classes, i.e., data-labels is used for selecting the data points; this technique gives good results for problems with large number of classes. Random sampling is widely used in mini-batching for large-scale learning problems (e.g., [19]), as only [34] is known importance sampling technique in mini-batching. In this chapter, we have focused on simple sampling techniques which do not involve any extra overhead and can be effective for dealing with large-scale learning problems. Two simple sampling techniques, namely, systematic sampling and cyclic/sequential sampling techniques have been proposed for selecting mini-batches. To the best of our knowledge, systematic sampling is not used in machine learning for selecting data points. We are the first to introduce systematic sampling in machine learning for the selection of mini-batches of data points from the dataset. Before this, cyclic sampling was used in coordinate descent and block coordinate descent methods (e.g.,[137]) for selecting one coordinate or block of coordinates, respectively, and to the best of our knowledge, cyclic sampling is not used for selecting mini-batches. Both sampling techniques are simple, effective and easy to implement. The proposed sampling techniques try to reduce the training time of models by reducing the data access time because these are based on contiguous access of data. Before this, for reducing the training time of models, the focus is mainly given on reducing the processing time, but this chapter has focused on reducing the data access time by changing the sampling techniques.

5.1.3  Contributions

The contributions of the article are summarized below:

  • Novel systematic sampling and sequential/cyclic sampling techniques have been proposed for selecting mini-batches of data points from the dataset for solving large-scale learning problems. The proposed techniques focus on reducing the training time of learning algorithms by reducing the data access time, and are based on simple observations that data stored on the contiguous memory locations are faster to access as compared with data stored on dispersed memory locations. To the best of our knowledge, this chapter is the first to focus on reducing data access time to reduce the overall training time for machine learning algorithms.

  • Proposed ideas are independent of problem and method used to solve problem as it focuses on data access only. So, it can be extended to other machine learning problems.

  • Experimental results prove the efficacy of systematic sampling and cyclic sampling in reducing the training time, and show up to six times faster training. The results have been provided using five different methods (SAG, SAGA, SVRG, SAAG-II and MBSGD) each using two step determination techniques (constant step size and backtracking line search methods) over eight bench marked datasets.

  • Theoretical results prove similar convergence for learning algorithms using cyclic and systematic sampling, as for widely used random sampling technique, in expectation.

5.2  Systematic Sampling

Sampling is the way of selecting one mini-batch of data points (or one data point) from the whole dataset. Iterative approximation methods used for solving the problem use sampling again and again during each iteration/epoch. The convergence of learning algorithm depends upon the type of sampling used since sampling controls two things: data access time and diversity of data. In general, when consecutive data points are used they reduce the data access time which reduces the training time but selected data points might not be diverse which affects the convergence of learning algorithm. On the other hand, when data is used from different locations then the data access time is more which increases the training time but selected data points might be diverse which can improve the convergence. Thus, sampling has a significant role in the learning algorithms. Three sampling techniques, namely, random sampling, cyclic sampling and systematic sampling are discussed below and used in learning algorithm as they are simple, easy to implement and do not involve any extra overhead and thus effective in handling large-scale problems.

5.2.1  Definitions

Suppose a mini-batch B of m data points is to be selected from a training dataset {(x1,y1),(x2,y2),...,(xl,yl)} of l data points.
